Exact local namespace/observer/domain binding is separate from captured lineage/H authorization. ===== docs/scratch/arrival-break/consistency-d0-d2-2026-09-06.md =====
1: # D0 → D2: explicit credential requests and persisted bindings
2: 
3: Status: implemented and validated on `arrival/finish`, based on `39bf05d7`;
4: awaiting final Fable-low implementation review. Uncommitted; nothing pushed.
5: All credentials and stores used for testing are disposable fixtures.
6: 
7: ## Result
8: 
9: `MappedCredentialProvider` is an explicit alternative to the existing custody
10: provider. Exact namespace/observer strings select a persisted opaque key
11: reference. FACT, ARRIVAL, and TICK requests carry distinct domains and purposes;
12: the engine separately captures and verifies the authorizing history. Ordinary,
13: packed-batch, source-tier, and declaration preparation now support that path.
14: 
15: Initialization loads a pre-created binding. Observer grants require an explicit
16: public key when using mapped credentials. Creation/import/reuse/recovery are
17: separate custody operations, with serialized mutation, durable pending intents,
18: and complete-file publication. Legacy flat/nested import retains ambiguity and
19: alias refusal and leaves the original key files untouched. No read, preview,
20: or signer load implicitly creates or imports a binding.
21: 
22: The default provider and raw legacy callbacks retain their compatibility
23: behavior. Mapped credentials refuse on legacy writers; partial mapped
24: configuration cannot fall back to legacy callbacks. Arrival wire formats and
25: initialization intent recovery remain unchanged. Public guidance is in
26: [SDK README](../../../libs/sdk/README.md) and
27: [custody README](../../../libs/custody/README.md).
28: 
29: ## Design review and implementation corrections
30: 
31: The [Fable-low design review](reviews/consistency-d0-d2-design-2026-09-06/design-findings.md)
32: returned **REVISE**. [Primary triage](reviews/consistency-d0-d2-design-2026-09-06/primary-triage.md)
33: accepted its three findings: retain self-key introduction under an older
34: valid key; choose one coherent pending-intent/concurrency protocol; and retain
35: unsigned receipts before the signed era when no receipt observer is configured.
36: The amended [design](consistency-d0-d2-design-2026-09-06.md) records these rules.
37: 
38: Independent root probes reproduced five failures in the first custody draft:
39: recovery without an intent minted a binding; completed creation skipped material
40: validation; reused tokens poisoned another slot; import recovery lost its
41: provenance; and a private-before-public interruption could not resume. A later
42: probe caught replacement-key creation during recovery of a missing published
43: key. All seven final recovery probes pass. These were development-draft probes,
44: not regressions against the pre-D0 checkpoint.
45: 
46: Integrated SDK validation caught five legacy kind-operation call sites missing
47: the newly required guard argument. Those were fixed and the full SDK passed.
48: A final malformed-evidence probe found a JSON serialization failure in refusal
49: reporting; public fields are now sanitized and four regressions pass.
50: 
51: ## Measured validation
52: 
53: - Full engine: **2,611 passed, 1 skipped**, 70.50 seconds.
54: - Final full SDK: **578 passed**, 23.97 seconds.
55: - Full custody: **67 passed**, 1.54 seconds.
56: - Full signing primitives: **40 passed**, 5.13 seconds.
57: - Architecture: **101 passed**, 7.57 seconds.
58: - Scoped Ruff across every changed Python file and `git diff --check`: pass.
59: 
60: The engine suite predates two test-only formatting corrections (import spacing
61: and identical adjacent SQL string literals); engine production did not change.
62: The final SDK suite includes the guard correction and malformed-evidence fix.
63: A combined custody/sign run with importlib collection failed to import the
64: multiprocessing test module in spawned children; the normal separate package
65: runs above pass. This harness failure is retained in the evidence.
66: 
67: Every test process used isolated `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and
68: `LOOPS_HOME`. Source hashes and raw logs are retained for review. See the
69: [validation report](consistency-d0-d2-validation-2026-09-06.md) for coverage and
70: limits. No real keys or live project stores were migrated.
71: 
72: ## Deliberate limits
73: 
74: - Mapping is opt-in; the default provider is still locator-based compatibility.
75: - A binding is domain-independent in v1; signatures remain domain-separated.
76: - Missing author bindings may remain unsigned where the current policy permits.
77: - Inner receipts use current declared keys, while authored records use verified
78:   lineage history through H for successor use. Outer ticks remain unsigned and
79:   retain the physical genesis custodian.
80: - Plans retain successful binding evidence, not a complete attempt audit.
81: - Key-history scanning is conditional on mapped credentials and may be O(N).
82: - The filesystem provider requires POSIX flock, hard-link, and fsync semantics.
83: - An import without a copied private candidate needs its source re-presented
84:   with the same token; a copied candidate can recover without the old source.
85: - No automatic key cleanup, local binding rotation, revocation, global identity,
86:   default CLI transition, or Arrival initialization-intent schema change.
87: - Exact retry signature equality remains the existing contract; rotation-aware
88:   retry ergonomics is a separate follow-up.
89: 
90: ## Review handoff
91: 
92: Final Fable-low implementation review pending. Its raw findings, primary triage,
93: source hashes, and any follow-up verification must be recorded before declaring
94: this slice complete. Sol owned engine integration, Terra owned custody/SDK,
95: Luna owned public-flow tests, and root owned integrated review and independent
96: recovery/refusal probes.

===== docs/scratch/arrival-break/consistency-d0-d2-design-2026-09-06.md =====
1: # D0/D2 explicit credential binding design
2: 
3: Status: accepted after Fable-low clarification and under implementation. The
4: design itself makes no key, store, or wire-format change.
5: 
6: ## Decision
7: 
8: A signing request has three independent identity inputs:
9: 
10: 1. an exact, explicitly configured local custody namespace;
11: 2. an exact protocol observer label; and
12: 3. an explicit signing domain.
13: 
14: The local binding key is `(namespace, observer)`. All currently supported
15: domains select the same opaque local key reference for that binding. The domain
16: is still present on every request and determines the signature prefix; a FACT
17: signature can never substitute for ARRIVAL or TICK. Domain-specific private-key
18: bindings are a future design, not an implicit option in v1.
19: 
20: The captured verification context is separate. For an existing lineage it
21: contains the captured full Head, successor position, and the keys authorized by
22: that lineage or effective declaration. It is constructed by the engine after
23: the registry has attested the target. It is never an input that lets the local
24: provider infer a namespace or choose another observer.
25: 
26: Equal observer strings in different namespaces do not share a binding.
27: Applications may deliberately bind an existing managed key reference into
28: another namespace, but that is an explicit mutation. Namespace, observer, and
29: public wire strings are compared byte-for-byte as Python strings: no casefold,
30: Unicode normalization, path splitting, or filename derivation.
31: 
32: ## Why the current interface is insufficient
33: 
34: `CredentialProvider.for_write(vertex: Path)` selects only from a mutable
35: locator. `CustodyCredentialProvider.for_write` eagerly constructs three
36: path-based callbacks, and custody derives the flat self observer from the file
37: stem. The callbacks expose neither the chosen key reference nor public-key
38: evidence. SDK preview, ordinary emit, batch emit, and sources all call this
39: interface before the engine captures H. Declaration edit does the same through
40: `_arrival_credentials`.
41: 
42: The runtime then calls those callbacks while planning FACT, ARRIVAL, and TICK
43: commitments. Ordinary runtime does not currently compare the selected local
44: author key with captured lineage key history. Declaration preparation does
45: perform such a check by scanning through captured H and consulting
46: `keys_valid_at(observer, H.ordinal + 1)`.
47: 
48: Initialization and observer grant bypass the provider entirely:
49: `init_vertex` and `grant_observer(key=None)` call
50: `ensure_signing_key`. This must not remain as a hidden legacy-key mutation
51: when a mapped provider is selected.
52: 
53: Relevant source seams:
54: 
55: - `engine.credentials:WriteCredentials, CredentialProvider`;
56: - `sdk.emit:CustodyCredentialProvider` and the preview/ordinary/batch calls;
57: - `sdk.sources:run_sources`;
58: - `sdk.declare:_arrival_credentials, _init_arrival`;
59: - `sdk.kind:grant_observer`;
60: - `engine.runtime_write:capture_runtime, plan_ordinary_write,
61:   plan_batch_from_capture`;
62: - `engine.arrival_sources:prepare_source_invocation,
63:   execute_source_invocation`;
64: - `engine.arrival_declarations:prepare_declaration_edit`; and
65: - `engine.arrival_initialization:initialize_arrival`.
66: 
67: ## Neutral engine API
68: 
69: The proposed values live beside `WriteCredentials` in
70: `engine.credentials`. They contain no filesystem or Ed25519 dependency.
71: 
72: ```python
73: class SigningDomain(StrEnum):
74:     FACT = "fact"
75:     ARRIVAL = "arrival"
76:     TICK = "tick"
77: 
78: class CredentialPurpose(StrEnum):
79:     AUTHORSHIP = "authorship"
80:     KEY_INTRODUCTION = "key-introduction"
81:     RECEIPT = "receipt"
82:     INITIALIZATION = "initialization"
83: 
84: @dataclass(frozen=True)
85: class CredentialRequest:
86:     namespace: str
87:     observer: str
88:     domain: SigningDomain
89:     purpose: CredentialPurpose
90: 
91: @dataclass(frozen=True)
92: class CredentialBindingEvidence:
93:     request: CredentialRequest
94:     key_ref: str
95:     algorithm: str
96:     public_key: str
97:     provenance: str
98: 
99: @dataclass(frozen=True)
100: class ResolvedCredential:
101:     evidence: CredentialBindingEvidence
102:     sign_digest: Callable[[str], str]
103: 
104: BindingResolver = Callable[[CredentialRequest], ResolvedCredential | None]
105: SignatureVerifier = Callable[[SigningDomain, str, str, str], bool]
106: 
107: class CredentialBindingRefused(ValueError):
108:     reason: str
109:     request: CredentialRequest | None
110:     captured_head: Head | None
111:     use_position: int | None
112:     evidence: CredentialBindingEvidence | None
113: ```
114: 
115: The request validates non-empty namespace and observer strings but does not
116: normalize them. `key_ref` is opaque outside the provider. Public outcomes may
117: serialize `CredentialBindingEvidence`; they never serialize the signer,
118: private-key location, or provider root.
119: 
120: `WriteCredentials` gains trailing, constructor-compatible optional fields:
121: 
122: ```python
123: binding_namespace: str | None = None
124: receipt_observer: str | None = None
125: binding_resolver: BindingResolver | None = None
126: signature_verifier: SignatureVerifier | None = None
127: ```
128: 
129: The four mapped fields are an all-or-none coherent mode except that
130: `receipt_observer` is required only when a TICK signature is requested.
131: Existing `tick_signer`, `fact_signer`, and `arrival_signer` remain the
132: legacy mode. If mapped mode is configured, the engine never falls back to a
133: legacy callback for a missing, corrupt, or unauthorized mapped binding.
134: 
135: `WriteCredentials.__post_init__` requires namespace, resolver, and verifier to
136: be all present or all absent; mapped mode and legacy signer callbacks are
137: mutually exclusive. Namespace and any supplied receipt observer must be exact
138: non-empty strings. A receipt observer without mapped mode is invalid. These
139: static configuration errors raise `ValueError`. When boundary planning produces
140: a tick without a receipt observer, no TICK request is constructed and the tick
141: remains unsigned where the captured era permits it. An established signed tick
142: era instead refuses with `CredentialBindingRefused(reason="missing-required")`.
143: 
144: Resolvers and verifiers are invoked as callables. A provider normally assigns
145: its bound `resolve` and `verify` methods to those fields. The typed refusal is a
146: neutral shared precommit cause; runtime, source, and declaration wrappers keep
147: their established public families and retain it through `cause`/exception
148: chaining. Its evidence never contains a private path.
149: 
150: A mapped SDK provider may still implement `for_write(Path)` for source
151: compatibility, but that method only returns this lazy configuration. It must
152: not load, create, or choose a key. The explicit namespace comes from provider
153: configuration, not from the Path argument.
154: 
155: An internal frozen `CapturedSigningContext` records:
156: 
157: - `captured_head`;
158: - `use_position = captured_head.ordinal + 1`;
159: - verified author-key introductions through H; and
160: - the effective declaration's current receipt-key set.
161: 
162: It remains separate from `CredentialRequest` and is not sent to the provider.
163: 
164: ## Engine resolution and verification rule
165: 
166: The engine signs in this order:
167: 
168: 1. Open and attest the descriptor, capture full H, effective declaration, and
169:    any required projection evidence.
170: 2. Only for mapped credentials, scan the structurally verified custody prefix
171:    through H and build selective key history using the injected signature
172:    verifier. Legacy and unsigned paths do not gain this new prerequisite.
173: 3. Construct the exact request for the commitment being planned.
174: 4. Resolve the binding. Verify that the returned request exactly equals the
175:    offered request and that its key reference, algorithm, and public key are
176:    well formed.
177: 5. Check the public key against the captured authorization policy below.
178: 6. Sign the digest, independently verify the signature using
179:    `signature_verifier(domain, public_key, signature, digest)`, then retain
180:    serializable binding evidence on the plan.
181: 7. Any mismatch refuses before append. It never downgrades a contradictory
182:    binding to unsigned output.
183: 
184: A missing binding is distinct from corruption. Where the existing protocol
185: allows an unsigned ordinary fact/envelope, `None` may retain that behavior
186: and the plan records an unbound/unsigned decision. Declaration edits,
187: initialization, and a TICK in an established signed tick era require a usable
188: binding. Alias, conflict, public-key mismatch, malformed state, or resolver
189: failure always refuses.
190: 
191: ### Authorization table
192: 
193: | Commitment | Request observer/domain | Captured authorization |
194: | --- | --- | --- |
195: | Inner fact, including each batch row | Exact fact author / FACT | That observer's verified lineage keys valid at H+1 |
196: | Outer fact or packed batch | Exact envelope author / ARRIVAL | That observer's verified lineage keys valid at H+1 |
197: | Declaration fact and envelope | Explicit edit author / FACT and ARRIVAL | Edit author's verified lineage keys valid at H+1 |
198: | Key introduction | Explicit introducing author / ARRIVAL | Introducing author's existing verified lineage keys valid at H+1. The introduced subject may equal the author, but the newly introduced key cannot authorize its own introducing record |
199: | Inner boundary tick | Explicit configured receipt observer / TICK | Returned public key is in the effective declaration's current declared receipt-key set, preserving the existing any-declared-key policy |
200: | Outer tick envelope | No signing request | Observer remains the verified physical genesis custodian and the envelope remains unsigned |
201: | Initialization declaration | Exact founding observer / FACT and ARRIVAL | Planned bootstrap context; both bindings must expose the same public key and verify independently |
202: | Physical genesis | Exact founding observer / ARRIVAL | Planned bootstrap context and requested founding public key |
203: 
204: For authored records, H+1 is sufficient even when a prepared batch expands to
205: several physical records: only keys introduced through H may authorize the
206: operation. A key-introduction draft in the same append cannot authorize a
207: sibling draft. The tick check uses keys declared in the same captured effective
208: declaration, not every key ever introduced into the physical lineage. The
209: request's receipt observer names local selection evidence; it does not change
210: the wire's existing any-declared-key receipt policy or add a signer identity to
211: the tick row.
212: 
213: Receipt-observer absence is evaluated only after planning proves a tick exists.
214: It constructs no TICK request and preserves unsigned pre-signed-era behavior;
215: it becomes `missing-required` only after captured ticks establish a signed era.
216: 
217: ## Runtime integration
218: 
219: `capture_runtime` accepts the mapped verifier configuration and conditionally
220: retains immutable key-history evidence on `RuntimeCapture`. New fields are
221: trailing/defaulted so existing direct test construction and legacy callers
222: remain compatible.
223: 
224: `prepare_ordinary_write` captures before resolving either author domain.
225: The low-level `plan_ordinary_write` may continue accepting legacy callbacks,
226: but mapped credentials without a captured signing context refuse rather than
227: sign without authorization evidence.
228: 
229: `prepare_batch_write` captures once. `plan_batch_from_capture` resolves
230: FACT and ARRIVAL for each exact author and TICK for the explicit receipt
231: observer. Repeated requests may be cached only within that one captured
232: planning operation and only by the complete request value.
233: 
234: Source invocation resolves nothing before initial capture. Preflight uses the
235: initial context. Later dependency tiers recapture H, rebuild key evidence, and
236: resolve again before planning that tier. A binding change after collection
237: therefore causes a retained known-uncommitted tier refusal; collectors are not
238: rerun. One credential resolution must not be cached across tier captures.
239: 
240: Declaration preparation keeps its existing full-prefix selective registry.
241: Its signer helper changes to the mapped request path when configured, retaining
242: the current H+1 author check and independent FACT/ARRIVAL signature
243: verification. A no-change declaration plan need not resolve a signer.
244: 
245: Preview performs the same captured checks and may compute signatures in memory,
246: but provider resolution is read-only: it cannot create a key, binding, intent,
247: directory, or convenience public-key file.
248: 
249: Public plans/results may add ordered evidence for bindings that resolved and
250: produced verified signatures. Missing bindings and refused attempts remain
251: explicit in the typed refusal/request evidence; this first slice does not claim
252: an audit log of every absent or unsigned decision. The additive successful
253: binding evidence is not a new identity claim.
254: 
255: ## Explicit mapped filesystem provider
256: 
257: The v1 mapped provider is separately configured with:
258: 
259: - a provider root;
260: - one exact custody namespace; and
261: - an optional exact receipt observer.
262: 
263: It does not accept a vertex-derived default namespace. `CustodyCredentialProvider`
264: remains the explicitly documented legacy adapter; C4's rejection of non-None
265: `key_dir` remains in force.
266: 
267: ### Storage
268: 
269: Filesystem slot names are hashes of length-framed UTF-8 bytes, never encodings
270: that are later treated as identity:
271: 
272: ```text
273: <provider-root>/
274:   bindings-v1/<sha256(frame(namespace, observer))>.json
275:   keys-v1/<opaque-key-ref>/ed25519.key
276:   keys-v1/<opaque-key-ref>/ed25519.pub
277:   intents-v1/<operation-token>.json
278: ```
279: 
280: A binding record contains at least:
281: 
282: ```json
283: {
284:   "schema": "loops.custody/binding/v1",
285:   "namespace": "<exact string>",
286:   "observer": "<exact string>",
287:   "key_ref": "<opaque provider ref>",
288:   "algorithm": "ed25519",
289:   "public_key": "<raw-32-byte base64>",
290:   "provenance": "created|legacy-import|existing-ref"
291: }
292: ```
293: 
294: The loader recomputes the slot hash from the exact stored strings, confines the
295: key reference beneath the provider root, refuses symlinks and non-regular
296: files, loads the private key, derives its public key, and compares it with the
297: binding. If `ed25519.pub` exists it must also match. A missing convenience
298: public file is not created during resolution. This avoids
299: `ed25519.load_or_generate` silently repairing a mismatched public file before
300: the binding can be checked.
301: 
302: A binding is domain-independent in v1. Every domain request for the same
303: namespace/observer returns the same key ref and public key while constructing a
304: domain-specific signer. A provider returning different refs across domains is
305: corrupt.
306: 
307: ### Atomic creation and concurrency
308: 
309: Every binding mutation—create, import, existing-ref bind, intent recovery and
310: explicit orphan maintenance—is serialized by one provider-root advisory lock.
311: Read-only resolution takes no lock and never persists. The provider refuses to
312: operate on a filesystem where it cannot obtain the configured lock semantics;
313: an in-process mutex alone is insufficient.
314: 
315: Creation requires an explicit operation token and follows a recoverable state
316: machine while holding that lock:
317: 
318: 1. Publish a complete, fsynced creation-intent file using a temp file plus
319:    no-clobber hard link, then fsync the parent. The intent fixes namespace,
320:    observer, candidate key ref, and creation/import source. A repeated token
321:    must match exactly.
322: 2. Create the candidate key object under its unique ref. Publish complete key
323:    files before any binding can name them and fsync the key directory.
324: 3. Build and fsync the complete binding JSON in a temporary file.
325: 4. Publish the binding slot with a no-clobber hard link and fsync its parent.
326:    Never create the final slot and then write into it.
327: 5. Mark the intent complete atomically. If the slot already exists, validate
328:    and return it; never replace it or delete any intent-retained candidate.
329: 
330: A crash before the intent has no durable effect. At most one pending intent may
331: exist for a namespace/observer slot. A crash after intent but before binding is
332: resumed with that intent's candidate key ref. A new call with a different token
333: must return recovery-required (or explicitly resume the named pending intent),
334: not generate a competing candidate. A crash after binding publication is
335: reconciled by comparing the slot with the intent.
336: 
337: The provider-wide lock means two live creators do not both generate candidates:
338: the second observes either the first pending intent or the completed binding.
339: A pre-existing binding with different requested public material is a conflict,
340: not a rotation. Candidate key objects retained by incomplete or losing intents
341: are never deleted automatically; an explicit locked maintenance operation may
342: classify them after checking all binding and intent references. Anonymous temp
343: files are removable. Rotation and revocation remain outside D2.
344: 
345: The pending intent is indexed by the same namespace/observer slot hash (for
346: example `pending-v1/<slot-hash>` naming its operation token), so discovery does
347: not depend on an unlocked directory scan. Publishing or removing that marker
348: and every intent transition occurs under the provider-root lock.
349: 
350: `bind_existing_ref(namespace, observer, key_ref, expected_public_key, token)`
351: supports deliberate reuse of one already-managed key across namespaces. It
352: performs the same validation and exclusive publication and never infers reuse
353: from equal observer labels.
354: 
355: ### Explicit legacy onboarding
356: 
357: Mapped resolution never scans `<vertex>/keys`. A separate explicit import
358: ceremony receives the legacy vertex path, exact observer, destination
359: namespace, and operation token. It:
360: 
361: 1. invokes the current flat/nested resolver without minting;
362: 2. preserves its missing-versus-alias/ambiguity distinction;
363: 3. derives public material from the private key and refuses a malformed or
364:    mismatched existing `.pub`;
365: 4. copies the private material into a new managed key object with restrictive
366:    permissions, leaving the legacy files untouched; and
367: 5. publishes one mapped binding through the creation state machine.
368: 
369: The copy is an explicit custody mutation and must be reported. It is not run by
370: reads, preview, signer loading, write fallback, locator move, or declaration
371: inspection. Existing labels that the path layout cannot represent cannot be
372: auto-imported; they can receive a new mapped binding through explicit creation.
373: After import, moving the vertex locator does not change the mapped selection.
374: 
375: ## Initialization and observer grant
376: 
377: Initialization has no captured existing H. It does not create a mapped binding.
378: The caller first completes the separate explicit creation/import operation,
379: then supplies that mapped provider to initialization. Initialization resolves
380: the existing binding read-only using a planned bootstrap context containing the
381: target, pending lineage and founding observer. This happens before the engine's
382: durable initialization intent, so documentation and results must call it
383: planned, not reserved.
384: 
385: Before resolving that pre-created binding, the SDK performs all available
386: zero-mutation checks: target/intent conflicts, descriptor and declaration
387: shape, identity collision, key format, and backend atomic capability.
388: Resolution itself remains read-only.
389: 
390: FACT and ARRIVAL requests must resolve the same public key. The existing engine
391: then verifies both domain signatures against that key before it reserves the
392: initialization intent. Recovery continues to use the exact signatures already
393: stored in that intent and signs nothing.
394: 
395: The first D2 slice should not change the v1 initialization-intent schema merely
396: to duplicate the custody receipt. Existing intents lack such a field and the
397: current observer, public key, and exact signatures already suffice for engine
398: recovery. The earlier binding result and slot are the durable local receipt.
399: Recovery must remain possible without re-resolving private custody; a later
400: mismatched binding affects future writes, not completion of an already reserved
401: bootstrap. Adding optional cross-links between the operations is a later
402: versioned evidence improvement.
403: 
404: When detectable, recovery reports both facts: initialization completed from
405: the reserved signatures, while a currently configured mismatched binding means
406: the next mapped write will refuse. Drift never makes recovery sign again or
407: abandon an already reserved bootstrap.
408: 
409: For `grant_observer(key=None)`, a mapped provider refuses before touching either
410: key layout and tells the caller to complete explicit binding creation first,
411: then pass that binding's public key. It must never call legacy
412: `ensure_signing_key`. Supplying an explicit public key continues not to imply
413: possession of its private key or create a local binding.
414: 
415: A separately created binding may survive a later declaration or initialization
416: refusal. That is expected composition of two durable operations, not a partial
417: effect hidden inside the later error.
418: 
419: ## Legacy compatibility and migration boundary
420: 
421: The following remain transitional behavior:
422: 
423: - `CustodyCredentialProvider()` and explicit `None` use current path/stem,
424:   flat-self, and exact nested-observer lookup;
425: - missing legacy material may yield unsigned output where currently legal;
426: - filesystem aliases, symlinks, unsafe components, and conflicting flat/nested
427:   self keys keep refusing;
428: - legacy `engine.handle` and `engine.ceremony` retain callback credentials
429:   through `CredentialProvider.for_write(Path)` and explicitly refuse mapped
430:   credentials because they cannot establish a captured binding context; and
431: - directly supplied legacy `WriteCredentials` retain callback semantics.
432: 
433: These paths do not claim stable D0 namespace binding. They receive no automatic
434: migration and no new key-history scan prerequisite. Documentation must say a
435: locator move is stable only for an explicitly mapped provider after onboarding.
436: 
437: Mapped mode is opt-in. Once selected for an operation, there is no fallback to
438: legacy directories or callbacks. This prevents a corrupt binding from being
439: laundered into an unsigned or differently keyed write.
440: 
441: `SigningDomain` is neutral vocabulary. Mapping FACT, ARRIVAL, and TICK to the
442: `loops-fact-v1`, `loops-arrival-v1`, and `loops-tick-v1` prefixes remains
443: exclusively in custody. The SDK can adapt the generic four-argument verifier to
444: the existing domain-specific three-argument declaration/initialization verifier
445: callbacks; engine never hardcodes custody prefixes.
446: 
447: ## Failure and result evidence
448: 
449: Introduce a typed precommit `CredentialBindingRefused` carrying the exact
450: request, captured Head/use position when present, reason code, and safe binding
451: evidence when available. It must not expose private paths. Suggested reasons
452: are `missing-required`, `corrupt-binding`, `request-mismatch`,
453: `unauthorized-public-key`, `signature-invalid`, `receipt-key-undeclared`,
454: and `recovery-required`.
455: 
456: Provider corruption and authorization disagreement refuse before signing or
457: append. Provider `BaseException` identity is preserved. Ordinary absence that
458: legally produces unsigned output is an explicit decision rather than an error.
459: Postcommit failure taxonomy is unchanged because credential work finishes
460: before append.
461: 
462: Creation/import results separately report operation token, winning key ref and
463: public key, whether a key object or binding was created, and whether another
464: creator won. Outcome-unknown errors retain the intent path/token and candidate
465: ref so callers reconcile instead of retrying with a new key.
466: 
467: ## Implementation order and acceptance
468: 
469: A bounded implementation should proceed in four implementation steps followed
470: by one completed-stage external review:
471: 
472: 1. Add neutral request/evidence types and lazy mapped fields while preserving
473:    constructors and all legacy behavior.
474: 2. Add the mapped filesystem provider and creation/import recovery tests,
475:    without routing SDK defaults to it.
476: 3. Integrate conditional captured verification into ordinary, batch, source,
477:    and declaration preparation; expose an explicit SDK mapped provider.
478: 4. Integrate initialization and observer grant with pre-created mapped bindings,
479:    retaining old initialization recovery and refusing hidden mapped creation.
480: 
481: Required tests include:
482: 
483: - exact case- and Unicode-distinct namespaces/observers without path semantics;
484: - FACT/ARRIVAL/TICK domain separation and same-key-ref v1 policy;
485: - captured H+1 authorization, unauthorized replacement refusal, and no
486:   within-batch introduction authorization;
487: - current-declaration any-key receipt validation distinct from author history;
488: - provider resolution only after H, and source re-resolution per later tier;
489: - preview/read/load causing zero persistence;
490: - missing unsigned versus corrupt refusal;
491: - complete-temp/no-clobber binding publication, concurrent winner, every crash
492:   phase, same-intent recovery, retained candidates, and explicit orphan maintenance;
493: - malformed/mismatched `.pub`, private/public mismatch, symlink and slot-payload
494:   mismatch refusal;
495: - explicit legacy flat/nested import preserving current ambiguity rules;
496: - deliberate existing-ref reuse across namespaces;
497: - locator move after mapped onboarding;
498: - initialization requires a pre-created binding, enforces the FACT/ARRIVAL
499:   same-public rule, and recovers old intents; and
500: - mapped `grant_observer(key=None)` refuses before effects and never performs
501:   hidden legacy key generation.
502: 
503: ## Judgment issues for review
504: 
505: 1. **Receipt observer configuration.** When receipt signing is requested, the
506:    design requires an explicit provider `receipt_observer`. It deliberately
507:    does not infer one from vertex name, boundary name/origin, fact author, or
508:    physical custodian. Omitting it retains unsigned pre-signed-era ticks and
509:    refuses once a signed era requires a receipt; a product surface must decide
510:    where users configure it.
511: 2. **Legacy onboarding copies private material.** Copying into managed storage
512:    makes locator moves stable and leaves the original untouched, but duplicates
513:    a secret. An external-reference key ref avoids duplication but does not
514:    survive moving the old key directory. This design chooses explicit secure
515:    copy and requires the operation to report it.
516: 3. **Initialization intent cross-link.** The first slice keeps its proven v1
517:    recovery format unchanged. A later schema may cross-link the custody intent
518:    for richer provenance, but recovery must remain compatible with old intents.
519: 4. **Unsigned mapped absence.** Existing ordinary unsigned behavior is retained.
520:    If the product wants selecting mapped mode to require signatures for every
521:    author, that is a separate policy flag; it must not be smuggled into D0.
522: 5. **Cost.** Verified key-history scanning is conditional on mapped signing and
523:    may be O(N). A backend-neutral authenticated key-history query can optimize
524:    it later without changing the request or authorization rule.

===== docs/scratch/arrival-break/consistency-d0-d2-validation-2026-09-06.md =====
1: # D0→D2 identity validation plan — 2026-09-06
2: 
3: This is an independent validation plan for the next credential-binding slice.
4: It does not ratify a wire migration or a filesystem layout. The authoritative
5: constraints are [identity decisions](identity-decisions-2026-09-06.md), the
6: [identity audit](consistency-audit-identity-2026-09-06.md), and the Arrival
7: wire/backend contracts.
8: 
9: ## Executed validation — implementation freeze
10: 
11: The matrix below began as an acceptance plan. The measured runs are:
12: 
13: | Suite | Result | Raw log in review validation evidence |
14: | --- | --- | --- |
15: | Engine | 2,611 passed, 1 skipped / 70.50s | engine-full.txt |
16: | SDK, final | 578 passed / 23.97s | sdk-full-final.txt |
17: | Custody | 67 passed / 1.54s | custody-full.txt |
18: | Sign | 40 passed / 5.13s | sign-full.txt |
19: | Architecture | 101 passed / 7.57s | architecture.txt |
20: | Changed Python Ruff | Passed | ruff-final.txt |
21: | Diff whitespace | Passed | diff-check.txt |
22: 
23: Root used process-level isolated state/config/Loops roots per suite, all beneath
24: `/tmp/loops-arrival-d0-d2-2026-09-06/validation`. Agent focused checks also used
25: isolated roots. No test intentionally addressed real credentials or live stores.
26: 
27: New public-flow tests in `test_arrival_mapped_credentials.py` passed 12 cases:
28: domain checks; initialization with a distinct observer; relocation; two-author
29: packed batches; receipt signatures; source-tier resolution and collector error
30: evidence; preview non-persistence; explicit grants; wrong public-key refusal;
31: relabeled bootstrap evidence; legacy refusal; and missing-reference refusal.
32: Some tests cover multiple assertions. These do not exhaust every hypothetical
33: case in the original matrix below.
34: 
35: Root's seven independent recovery tests initially reproduced draft failures
36: and then passed. Four malformed-public-evidence tests ensure refusal output is
37: JSON-serializable. Existing engine tests were extended for mapped author and
38: receipt policy, captured contexts, declaration signing, and legacy refusal.
39: 
40: The first full SDK run had 40 failures and 7 setup errors from one omitted
41: keyword argument at five SDK kind guard call sites; all were resolved before
42: the final full run. Combined custody/sign importlib collection caused spawned
43: workers to fail importing their test module; separate normal package collection
44: passed. Both initial failures remain in the raw evidence.
45: 
46: Two engine test formatting changes followed its full run; production was
47: unchanged. The final SDK run includes the later SDK guard and serialization
48: fixes. Hash records distinguish initial validation from the review freeze.
49: The final external review remains pending; passing tests are not its verdict.
50: 
51: ## Contract under test
52: 
53: D0 separates four values that must not be inferred from one another:
54: 
55: 1. the exact protocol observer label;
56: 2. the provider's explicit custody namespace and opaque key reference;
57: 3. the signing domain (FACT, ARRIVAL, or tick); and
58: 4. the verification context, including lineage and the captured successor
59:    coordinate at which a key was valid.
60: 
61: D2 persists the provider's mapping from exact observer identity to opaque key
62: references. It keeps the existing flat self-key and nested observer behavior
63: as compatibility inputs, while refusing ambiguous aliases, conflicting
64: public material, unsafe path components, and silent winner selection. A
65: filename, vertex stem, descriptor location, lineage, or equal observer label
66: across lineages is not a key-history proof.
67: 
68: ## Adversarial matrix
69: 
70: ### Request and domain separation (D0)
71: 
72: - A fact, batch, and declaration edit request credentials with explicit
73:   observer/domain context. A provider must never substitute the vertex name,
74:   boundary name, founding observer, or location for the requested observer.
75:   An inner tick commitment uses the TICK domain and has no author label; its
76:   receipt-signing capability follows the established any-key policy. The
77:   outer Arrival envelope is unsigned and carries the physical log custodian.
78: - `WriteCredentials` must preserve independent FACT, ARRIVAL, and tick
79:   callbacks. Missing one callback remains the operation's existing unsigned
80:   posture where allowed; another callback must not be used as a fallback.
81:   Verify each signature under its own domain and assert cross-domain
82:   verification fails.
83: - A provider is queried fresh per operation. Reads, previews, verification,
84:   signer loading, and failed preflight must not generate a private key or
85:   publish a binding. A failed signer/public-key relationship must refuse
86:   before intent, mint, or append.
87: - A key introduced at captured H is valid for a successor record at H+1, not
88:   retroactively for H. Test valid and invalid introduction coordinates,
89:   unknown observer, wrong lineage, and a signer whose bytes do not match the
90:   presented public evidence. Preserve the actual captured H and cause in the
91:   typed refusal.
92: - A tick's inner TICK signer and outer Arrival custodian label remain
93:   distinct. Test a tick signed by a non-genesis key and confirm the outer
94:   envelope still follows the established custodian rule. For facts, test an
95:   author different from the physical genesis observer. Equal labels in
96:   separate lineages must not be reported as globally authenticated identity.
97: 
98: ### Mapping and compatibility (D2)
99: 
100: - Existing flat `keys/ed25519.key` remains the self-observer compatibility
101:   key. Existing exact nested `keys/<observer>/` keys remain usable. Moving or
102:   renaming the `.vertex` file must not silently select a new key for the same
103:   explicit observer when a persisted binding exists.
104: - Exact nested labels, slash namespaces, case variants, Unicode, empty
105:   components, dot/parent components, reserved key filenames, symlinks, and
106:   regular-file path components must be tested. Preserve the current refusal
107:   policy; never borrow a case-folded or traversal-equivalent key.
108: - A mapping whose observer/namespace/reference is malformed, whose public
109:   material disagrees with the key, or whose target key is missing must refuse
110:   without overwriting existing bytes, minting a replacement, or choosing a
111:   newer arbitrary row. A mapping for one lineage is not silently reused as
112:   proof for another lineage unless the provider explicitly declares that
113:   namespace reuse and the operation still verifies lineage key history.
114: - Concurrent creators for the same exact binding must converge on one
115:   complete winner or return a typed conflict. They must not publish a partial
116:   mapping, two private keys, or a mapping pointing at the losing key. Inject
117:   interruption after key creation, after mapping temp write, and after rename;
118:   recovery must retain enough evidence to reconcile without generating a new
119:   identity.
120: - A provider configured with an unsupported key root must fail explicitly
121:   before signer lookup. The current `CustodyCredentialProvider(key_dir=...)`
122:   refusal is the compatibility baseline until an explicit provider/key-locator
123:   design is implemented; callers must not believe the argument was honored.
124: 
125: ### Operation and error compatibility
126: 
127: - Existing unsigned/pre-signature behavior remains legal where the operation
128:   permits it. A missing local key on a read or signer load must not mint one.
129: - Precommit input/configuration errors retain zero-mutation evidence. An
130:   append after credentials are acquired retains stable fact/tick IDs, captured
131:   head, and the proper committed, unwitnessed, projection-failed, or unknown
132:   outcome. Credential lookup failure must not be mislabeled append-unknown.
133: - Public error/outcome serialization must expose enough namespace, observer,
134:   domain, key-reference/public-evidence status, captured verification
135:   coordinate, and mutation phase to explain a refusal or uncertain result,
136:   without leaking private key bytes or reducing a committed outcome to a
137:   generic refusal. Success DTOs need only expose the identity evidence their
138:   operation contract promises; this plan does not require every result to
139:   repeat provider-internal references.
140: - Registration and custom-provider paths must preserve opaque backend
141:   locations and the caller's registry. Credential configuration must not
142:   derive identity from an adapter DSN or fall back to the built-in provider.
143: 
144: ## Existing evidence to extend
145: 
146: The current baseline already covers domain-separated custody signers and
147: flat/nested compatibility in `libs/custody/tests/test_signing.py`, including
148: alias, symlink, traversal, ambiguity, missing-load, and cross-domain cases.
149: `libs/sdk/tests/test_emit.py` covers operation-fresh `WriteCredentials`,
150: unsigned behavior, and the explicit refusal of the ineffective `key_dir`
151: override. `libs/sdk/tests/test_arrival_init.py` covers injected separate
152: fact/Arrival signers, wrong-domain and key mismatch preflight, partial custom
153: credentials, and recovery without generating a replacement key. These tests
154: are the compatibility floor; D0/D2 additions should prove persisted binding
155: selection and concurrency rather than mirror helper internals.
156: 
157: ## Proposed focused test split (pending API freeze)
158: 
159: Do not add these files until the provider request and binding result types are
160: settled. The likely ownership split is:
161: 
162: - `libs/custody/tests/test_binding.py`: persisted observer-to-opaque-reference
163:   selection, flat/nested compatibility, exact case/slash/Unicode labels,
164:   traversal/symlink/regular-file rejection, mismatched public material,
165:   concurrent creators, and interruption at key or mapping publication. Each
166:   case should assert prior bytes survive and that a loser cannot replace a
167:   complete winner.
168: - `libs/engine/tests/test_arrival_credentials.py`: engine-facing request
169:   context and verification only. Cover separate FACT/ARRIVAL/TICK domains,
170:   TICK's authorless inner commitment plus unsigned outer custodian envelope,
171:   key validity at H versus H+1, wrong lineage/unknown author, and the
172:   distinction between credential/preflight refusal and append-unknown. Use
173:   injected callbacks or signed temporary records; do not import custody
174:   layout helpers into the engine package.
175: - `libs/sdk/tests/test_arrival_mapped_credentials.py` (with compatibility
176:   assertions remaining in `test_emit.py` and `test_arrival_init.py`): public
177:   provider registration,
178:   moved vertex residence, opaque backend namespace collision refusal, fresh
179:   provider calls, no key generation on reads/previews, unsupported key-root
180:   override, and JSON-safe refusal/uncertain-result evidence. A success DTO
181:   should assert only the identity fields promised by its operation contract.
182: 
183: Sol's provisional neutral seam gives these cases concrete request points:
184: `SigningDomain`, `CredentialRequest(namespace, observer, domain)`, and
185: `CredentialBinding(request, key_ref, public_key, sign_digest)`, plus provider
186: namespace/receipt-observer/resolve/verifier operations. The tests should use
187: that seam when frozen rather than reach into custody filenames. For a TICK
188: request, the inner signature is TICK-domain evidence without an author label;
189: the outer receipt remains unsigned physical-custodian evidence. The request
190: shape must not turn either into a FACT-author assertion.
191: 
192: The minimum cross-operation acceptance sequence is: create an isolated real
193: lineage; bind two exact observers; introduce one observer at a known H; sign a
194: fact and a TICK-domain inner record with their required callbacks; verify the
195: unsigned outer envelope's custodian; then move the vertex residence and repeat
196: the lookup. Separate negative cases must prove that a FACT callback cannot
197: stand in for ARRIVAL or TICK, H is not retroactively authorized, an opaque
198: provider key is not interpreted as a local path, and a failed preflight creates
199: no key, mapping, intent, or append. These are contract tests, not requirements
200: to expose provider-internal references on every successful result.
201: 
202: The strongest conformance fixture should create a real temporary lineage,
203: introduce one or more observer keys at known ordinals, and perform a signed
204: fact, signed declaration/tick inner record, and Arrival envelope. It should
205: verify every signature against the historical registry and then repeat after
206: moving the vertex residence or changing the provider's local path. A separate
207: fake provider should prove opaque namespaces and collision refusal without
208: filesystem interpretation.
209: 
210: ## Validation protocol and limits
211: 
212: Every test process must set isolated `XDG_STATE_HOME`, `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`, and
213: `LOOPS_HOME` beneath a disposable task directory. Test setup must assert those
214: roots before creating keys or opening a store; no test may use the developer's
215: default custody state. Capture before/after hashes for provider and SDK files,
216: raw focused/full-suite output, architecture checks, and scoped Ruff.
217: 
218: No test in this plan moves or deletes real keys, rewrites live stores, invents
219: global identity equivalence, adds revocation semantics, or changes the Arrival
220: wire profile. Durable alias transfer, cross-lineage identity associations,
221: and a complete migration from filename lookup remain separate designs. The
222: first implementation should be accepted only when a failed or interrupted
223: binding operation leaves an honest typed result and preserves all prior key and
224: mapping bytes.

===== docs/scratch/arrival-break/reviews/consistency-d0-d2-design-2026-09-06/primary-triage.md =====
1: # D0/D2 design review — primary triage
2: 
3: Fable-low returned **REVISE** on the frozen design packet
4: `bacca7225908defa9df9c38ac75b1eca8cab2a34dd0d367fe0250f754a202769`.
5: The three design findings are accepted and resolved as implementation requirements;
6: the raw verdict is not rewritten as an external ACCEPT. The final implementation
7: review must assess these requirements in code and tests.
8: 
9: 1. **Self-key introduction:** the subject may equal the introducing author.
10:    Existing authorized key K1 may introduce K2 for the same exact observer.
11:    K2 may not authorize its own introduction. The original table wording was
12:    incorrect, not a reason to change the existing additive-key protocol.
13: 2. **Concurrency and interruption:** all provider mutations serialize under a
14:    provider-wide filesystem lock. A durable pending slot/token lookup identifies
15:    interrupted operations, which resume the same candidate or explicitly refuse.
16:    Binding publication uses a complete fsynced temporary file and no-clobber
17:    link. No automatic candidate deletion or unlocked reference-scan cleanup.
18:    Read-only resolution creates neither locks nor directories. Explicit
19:    maintenance/garbage collection is outside this implementation.
20: 3. **Receipt signer absence:** no configured receipt observer means no TICK
21:    request. An unsigned tick remains legal before the signed-tick era. Existing
22:    signed-era policy requires a usable receipt capability and refuses preappend
23:    when absent. No implicit receipt identity from author, boundary, or custodian.
24: 
25: Additional primary decisions:
26: 
27: - Mapped initialization loads a binding explicitly prepared by a separate
28:   custody create/import operation. It creates no binding itself. Existing
29:   initialization recovery signs nothing and remains usable without custody.
30: - Mapped observer grant with no explicit public key refuses before effects;
31:   callers prepare a binding separately and pass its public key. This is the
32:   explicit pre-effect refusal option in the design, avoiding mixed custody and
33:   declaration partial effects. Legacy default behavior remains unchanged.
34: - Enforce coherent mapped configuration when constructing WriteCredentials;
35:   partial configuration cannot silently select legacy callbacks.
36: - SigningDomain-to-prefix composition stays in custody. Engine uses injected
37:   verification and neutral values only.
38: - Keep the existing exact-retry signature comparison in this slice. The
39:   review's retry ergonomics suggestion is a separate follow-up, not a reason
40:   to silently weaken equality during the binding migration.
41: - Recovery must not inspect current private bindings to claim readiness for
42:   future writes. Completion reports established initialization facts; later
43:   writes independently check their current binding against captured history.
44: - The design's implementation ordering is four dependent steps within this
45:   implementation stage, followed by one Fable-low implementation review.
46: 
47: No production changes were made before this review returned. These are concrete
48: contract refinements, so implementation may proceed without another external
49: design round; final adversarial review and independent tests remain required.

===== libs/custody/README.md =====
1: # custody
2: 
3: Loops signing composition — the store's at-rest signing format, below every writer.
4: 
5: Owns the `loops-tick-v1` / `loops-fact-v1` domain-separation constants, the
6: custody-co-located `keys/` layout (per-observer nesting + flat self-observer
7: back-compat), the signer/verifier builders, and `ensure_signing_key`. Observer
8: names remain exact identities: a filesystem spelling collision refuses rather
9: than borrowing another observer's key. A renamed vertex may retain a nested
10: self key; all signer domains and `ensure_signing_key` select that one key.
11: Identical flat and nested self copies are one self key; conflicting key material
12: refuses until repaired. A differently spelled nested observer remains a
13: distinct observer and is never used as the self fallback. Observer components
14: cannot name the reserved `ed25519.key` or `ed25519.pub` custody files when
15: mapped to directories. This is a local custody-layout restriction, not an
16: observer label restriction in the event protocol. A self observer with such a
17: stem uses only the flat layout.
18: 
19: Signer loading never creates keys. Concurrent explicit key creation publishes
20: one complete private key; all callers load that key and derive its public file.
21: 
22: Any client that emits signed facts or ticks must compose identically or
23: `sl store verify` reports breaks — that's why this is a lib, not CLI code.
24: Depends on `sign` (loops-agnostic Ed25519 primitives) and `engine`
25: (store-canonical declaration resolution). The engine itself never imports
26: this — it takes signer callables by injection.
27: 
28: 
29: ## Explicit mapped bindings
30: 
31: `MappedCredentialProvider(root, namespace=..., receipt_observer=...)` provides
32: an opt-in alternative to locator-based custody. Exact namespace/observer
33: strings select an opaque managed key reference recorded with its public key.
34: All three signing domains use that binding with separate domain prefixes.
35: Hashed filesystem slots are storage details; labels retain their exact case,
36: Unicode spelling, and non-path characters. A binding does not authorize an
37: Arrival claim by itself: the engine separately checks captured history.
38: 
39: `for_write` returns lazy configuration. `resolve` loads and validates the
40: binding, private-derived public material, and any existing public convenience
41: file without creating or repairing anything. Missing unbound identities can
42: remain unsigned; malformed mappings, conflicting public material, missing
43: referenced private keys, and filesystem aliases refuse.
44: 
45: Explicit mutations are `create_binding(observer, token=...)`,
46: `import_legacy(vertex_path, observer, token=...)`, and
47: `bind_existing_ref(observer, key_ref, expected_public_key, token=...)`.
48: Import copies private material into managed custody and preserves the original
49: files. Reusing a managed reference is deliberate and does not introduce its
50: public key into an Arrival lineage. Existing bindings are not silently
51: replaced; local rotation is a separate future operation.
52: 
53: Mutations use a provider-wide POSIX advisory lock, durable intents, and
54: complete-file publication with no-clobber links. The supported filesystem must
55: provide those lock, link, and fsync semantics. Concurrent creators converge
56: on one binding; resolution remains read-only. There is no automatic orphan
57: cleanup or key deletion.
58: 
59: Keep the operation token when a mutation is interrupted.
60: `BindingMutationIncomplete` retains namespace, observer, token, candidate key
61: reference, and phase; the binding may already have been published.
62: `recover_binding(observer, token=...)` reconciles the retained intent and
63: candidate. It never replaces a missing key referenced by a published binding.
64: An import interrupted before copying private material needs the original
65: source re-presented with the same import token and expected public material;
66: recovery cannot invent that key. A completed imported candidate can recover
67: without the old source directory. Retrying with a different token does not
68: bypass an unfinished operation. Creation results report the returned binding
69: and effects of that invocation; they contain no private key material.

95:     "prepare_ordinary_write",

101:     """The runtime writer cannot establish a safe pre-append plan."""

104: class BoundaryContinuityRefused(RuntimeWriteRefused):
105:     """Current replay would consume an unlicensed historical boundary edge."""

107:     def __init__(
108:         self,
109:         *,
110:         issue: Any,
111:         basis: ReadBasis,

194: class WriteCommitUnknown(Exception):
195:     """Append raised without proving that it left custody unchanged.
196: 
197:     The stable planned identities let callers reconcile this operation without
198:     treating an adapter failure as permission to append the fact again.
199:     """
200: 
201:     outcome = "unknown"
202: 
203:     def __init__(self, plan: OrdinaryWritePlan, cause: Exception) -> None:
204:         super().__init__(
205:             "ordinary append outcome is unknown; reconcile by captured head "
206:             "and planned record identities"
207:         )
208:         self.captured_head = plan.captured_head
209:         self.fact_id = plan.fact_id
210:         self.tick_id = plan.tick_id
211:         self.drafts = plan.drafts
212:         self.cause = cause

215: class BatchWriteCommitUnknown(Exception):
216:     """A batch append raised without proving that custody stayed unchanged."""
217: 
218:     outcome = "unknown"
219: 
220:     def __init__(self, plan: BatchWritePlan, cause: Exception) -> None:
221:         super().__init__(
222:             "batch append outcome is unknown; reconcile by captured head "
223:             "and every planned record identity"
224:         )
225:         self.captured_head = plan.captured_head
226:         self.items = plan.items
227:         self.fact_ids = tuple(item.fact_id for item in plan.items)
228:         self.tick_ids = plan.pending_tick_ids + tuple(
229:             item.tick_id for item in plan.items if item.tick_id is not None
230:         )
231:         self.tick_runs = plan.pending_tick_runs + tuple(
232:             item.tick_run for item in plan.items if item.tick_id is not None
233:         )
234:         self.drafts = plan.drafts
235:         self.cause = cause

238: def _close_quietly(handle: object) -> None:
239:     close = getattr(handle, "close", None)
240:     if callable(close):
241:         with suppress(Exception):
242:             close()
